. We want to prove that
|f|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}=\max_{w\in\mathcal{S}}w(f)
. Let
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}
be the non-leading coefficients of the minimal polynomial of
f
over the fraction field of
A
. Choose
i_{0}
such that

|a_{i_{0}}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|a_{i}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}.

By Proposition
8.20
,
|f|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}=|a_{i_{0}}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}
. Since
A
is strongly
\varpi
-Shilov, Theorem
6.20
guarantees the existence of some
v\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{A})
with
\mathfrak{p}={\rm sp}(v)
a minimal prime ideal over
\varpi
such that
|a_{i_{0}}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}=v(a_{i_{0}})
.

Let
\mathfrak{q}
be any prime ideal of
B
lying over
\mathfrak{p}
. Since
A
,
B
are integrally closed in
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
, the local rings
A_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
B_{\mathfrak{q}}
are integrally closed in
A_{\mathfrak{p}}[\varpi^{-1}]
,
B_{\mathfrak{q}}[\varpi^{-1}]
. By Proposition
8.1
(2) applied to the integral extension

A_{\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow B_{\mathfrak{q}},

we see that the
\varpi
-adic completion
\widehat{B_{\mathfrak{q}}}
of
B_{\mathfrak{q}}
is a valuation ring of rank
1
, so
\mathfrak{q}
is minimal over
\varpi
. Let
v_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
w_{\mathfrak{q}}
be the restrictions to
A_{\mathfrak{p}}[\varpi^{-1}]
,
B_{\mathfrak{q}}[\varpi^{-1}]
of the
\varpi
-normalized rank-
1
valuations on
\widehat{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}[\varpi^{-1}]
,
\widehat{B_{\mathfrak{q}}}[\varpi^{-1}]
corresponding to the rank-
1
valuation rings
\widehat{A_{\mathfrak{p}}}
,
\widehat{B_{\mathfrak{q}}}
. Then Lemma
4.28
(together with
[
5
]
, Theorem 1.3.1) shows that

v_{\mathfrak{p}}=|\cdot|_{{\rm sp},A_{\mathfrak{p}},\varpi},w_{\mathfrak{q}}=|\cdot|_{{\rm sp},B_{\mathfrak{q}},\varpi}.

By Lemma
8.14
, this implies in particular that
w_{\mathfrak{q}}
is an extension of
v_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Thus there exists an element
w\in\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{B})
extending
v
with
{\rm sp}(w)=\mathfrak{q}
, namely, we let
w
be the restriction of
w_{\mathfrak{q}}
along the canonical map
B[\varpi^{-1}]\to B_{\mathfrak{q}}[\varpi^{-1}]
. Note that
v_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
w_{\mathfrak{q}}
have centers
\mathfrak{p}A_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\mathfrak{q}B_{\mathfrak{q}}
on
A_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
B_{\mathfrak{q}}
, so
w_{\mathfrak{q}}(\frac{g}{s})=w(g)
for every
g\in B
and
s\in B\setminus\mathfrak{q}
and
v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\frac{h}{t})=v(h)
for every
h\in A
and
t\in A\setminus\mathfrak{p}
. If
F
is the minimal polynomial of
f
over
{\rm Frac}(A)
, then
F
is also the minimal polynomial of
\frac{f}{1}\in B_{\mathfrak{q}}
over
{\rm Frac}(A)={\rm Frac}(A_{\mathfrak{p}})
. By Proposition
8.20
applied to the integral extension
A_{\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow B_{\mathfrak{q}}
and the spectral seminorms
|\cdot|_{{\rm sp},A_{\mathfrak{p}},\varpi}=v_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
|\cdot|_{{\rm sp},B_{\mathfrak{q}},\varpi}=w_{\mathfrak{q}}
, we see that

w(f)=w_{\mathfrak{q}}(\frac{f}{1})=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\frac{a_{i}}{1})=\max_{1\leq i\leq n}v(a_{i})\geq v(a_{i_{0}}).

But
v(a_{i_{0}})=|a_{i_{0}}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}=|f|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}
, by our choice of
i_{0}
and
v
, so that
w(f)\geq|f|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}
. This proves that
B
is strongly
\varpi
-Shilov if
B
is integrally closed in
\mathcal{B}
and
A
is strongly
\varpi
-Shilov.

It remains to prove that
B
is strongly
\varpi
-Krull if
B
is integrally closed in
\mathcal{B}
and
A
is strongly
\varpi
-Krull. By what we have proved above, we already know that
B
is strongly
\varpi
-Shilov. But
B
is also weakly
\varpi
-Krull, by Proposition
8.3
, so it must be strongly
\varpi
-Krull.∎

Corollary 8.23
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
be an extension of uniform Tate rings such that the integral closure
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{A}
in
\mathcal{B}
is a domain and is dense in
\mathcal{B}
. Suppose that the integral closure
\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}
of
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
inside
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is an open subring of
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
. If
\mathcal{A}
is an affinoid algebra in the sense of Tate over some nonarchimedean field, then
\mathcal{B}
satisfies Berkovich’s description of the Shilov boundary in the strong sense.

Proof.

Using Noether normalization for affinoid algebras, we may assume
\mathcal{A}
is a normal domain. Let
\varpi\in\mathcal{A}
be a topologically nilpotent unit. Note that

\mathcal{B}^{+}=\widehat{\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}}

(
\varpi
-adic completion) is an open and integrally closed subring of
\mathcal{B}
(it is open since
\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}
was assumed to be open in
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
). Since
\mathcal{B}
is uniform, this implies that
\mathcal{B}^{+}
is a ring of definition of
\mathcal{B}
. By Theorem
6.20
and Berkovich’s
[
5
]
, Proposition 2.4.4,
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
is strongly
\varpi
-Shilov. By Theorem
8.22
,
\mathcal{B}^{+}
is strongly
\varpi
-Shilov. By Theorem
6.20
once again, this implies that
\mathcal{B}
satisfies Berkovich’s description of the Shilov boundary.

It remains to prove that
{\rm sp}^{-1}({\rm Min}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}}(\varpi))
is closed in
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})=\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{B})
. Write
\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}
as a direct limit of finite subextensions
C_{i}
of
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
. By
[
28
]
, Corollary 1.1.15, each of the finitely generated
\mathcal{A}
-modules
\mathcal{C}_{i}=C_{i}[\varpi^{-1}]
is complete for its natural topology. Since
C_{i}
is finitely generated as an
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
-module, the natural topology on
\mathcal{C}_{i}
coincides with the topology given by the pair of definition
(C_{i},\varpi)
. Thus each
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, with topology defined by the pair
(C_{i},\varpi)
, is an affinoid algebra in the sense of Tate. Let
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{+}
be the integral closure of
C_{i}
in
\mathcal{C}_{i}
. By Proposition
8.21
, or by
[
9
]
, Theorem 6.3.5/1,
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{+}=\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\circ}
. By Berkovich’s description of the Shilov boundary for affinoid algebras (
[
5
]
, Proposition 2.4.4),
{\rm sp}^{-1}({\rm Min}_{\mathcal{C}_{i}^{+}}(\varpi))
is equal to the Shilov boundary for
\mathcal{C}_{i}
and thus closed. By Lemma
8.9
and Lemma
8.10
, this implies that
{\rm sp}^{-1}({\rm Min}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}}(\varpi))
is closed in
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime})
, as required. ∎

Corollary 8.24
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\subseteq\mathcal{B}
be an extension of uniform Tate rings, with topologically nilpotent unit
\varpi\in\mathcal{A}
, such that the integral closure
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{A}
in
\mathcal{B}
is dense in
\mathcal{B}
. Suppose that the integral closure
\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}
of
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
inside
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
is an open subring of
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
. Suppose that
\mathcal{A}
is a normal domain, that
\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
is coherent and that

|\mathcal{A}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}\subseteq\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}^{\times,m}|_{{\rm sp},\varpi}}\cup\{0\}.

Then
\mathcal{B}
satisfies Berkovich’s description of the Shilov boundary.

Proof.

We see as in the first paragraph of the proof of Corollary
8.23
that the
\varpi
-adic completion
\mathcal{B}^{+}
of
\mathcal{B}^{\prime+}
is a ring of definition of
\mathcal{B}
, so the assertion is equivalent to
\mathcal{B}^{+}
being strongly
\varpi
-Shilov. We conclude by combining Theorem
7.15
and Theorem
8.22
. ∎
